  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 4
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 8
        - Boldness: 7
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 6
        - WarmongerHate: 3

        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 4
        - MinorCivWarBias: 1
        - DenounceWillingness: 7
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 8
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (INCREASE Shoshone urgency - Moson Kahni produced Atomic Bomb this turn. Boldness increased to 7 (from 6) - more aggressive response needed to nuclear threat. HostileBias 6 (from 5) - Shoshone escalations require stronger diplomatic signaling. DiplomaticBalance reduced to 5 (from 6) - priority shifting toward military solutions over negotiation. Shoshone now has both cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced) AND nuclear capability. Must accelerate conquest or face nuclear retaliation. Naval battles with Shoshone (lost 2 Battleships) show their naval strength must be countered.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Electronics
        - Rationale: Computers already researched (completed this turn), Electronics is next unlocked. Electronics allows Police Station construction, improving city security levels against enemy spies and espionage. Critical protection for high-value science cities (Paris: 346 science/turn) and production centers. Also unlocks path toward Advanced Ballistics (more nuclear weapons) and alternative energy technologies. Security essential as Shoshone (355 tourism) and rivals likely stepping up espionage. Police Stations also provide culture synergy with Iron Fist tenet.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Ottoman relationship to neutral (0/0 from -50/-50). Ottomans eliminated with Istanbul captured - no longer a relevant diplomatic entity. No hostility modifiers needed as they pose no threat with only 1 remaining city (Bursa) and their civilization effectively destroyed. Maintaining any negative modifiers is unnecessary and could potentially affect diplomatic calculations with other civs who may have relationships with the Ottomans. This signals our peaceful stance toward the remaining Ottoman territory while focusing on consolidation and cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 156
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Brazil relationship to neutral (0/0 from 15/15). Brazil is capitulated Shoshone vassal and irrelevant to World Congress (only 6 votes). Resources better focused on Poland, Siam, World Congress diplomacy, and domestic production. Brazil poses no threat (1515 military vs France 7017) and won't impact primary cultural threat from Shoshone or Decolonization vote. Maintaining positive modifiers wastes diplomatic capacity on a defeated opponent.
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Edinburgh
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Edinburgh (city-state ally of Shoshone) declared war on France this turn due to Sphere of Influence over Palatium. Set relationship to highly hostile (-50/-50). Edinburgh only provides 1 vote to Shoshone, so diplomatic penalty is minimal. However, this sends a clear message about consequences of attacking France. Edinburgh is too distant (Shoshone's puppet city-state) to launch meaningful attacks. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response if Edinburgh's navy approaches (Shoshone has 2 city-states under their control: Brandenburg and Edinburgh).
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: STRENGTHEN SIAM ALLIANCE to 15/15 from 10/10. Siam remains France's only reliable vassal (5 World Congress delegates, voluntary submission). Increased positive relationship ensures Siam's continued support against England shared war and Poland secondary threat. Siam provides Lampang base for operations and valuable flank position. France needs maximum diplomatic capital during England war and World Congress voting (Decolonization threat in 7 turns). Stronger Siam bond demonstrates commitment to Allies, encouraging other civ consideration while France achieves England capitulation. Resources shift from Poland to England war - Siam becomes more critical strategic partner.
        - UpdatedTurn: 383
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Onondaga (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Onondaga declared war on France this turn, alongside Sokoto. Though small military threat, they are England-aligned ally in England-France war. Hostile posture ensures AI prioritizes defensive response and signals solidarity with English opposition. With increased defenses (Antiair 90, Air 95), France can handle these declarations without disrupting primary England/Shoshone strategy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: REDUCE relationship with Sokoto (English ally) to -25/-25 from 0/0. Sokoto declared war on France this turn along with Onondaga. Both are England-aligned city-state allies joining England's side in England-France war. Moderate hostility (-25/-25) signals France's opposition to England-aligned coalition without extreme measures. Sokoto and Onondaga are secondary threats; primary focus remains England nuclear threat and Shoshone cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE hostility vs Poland to -25/-25 from -15/-15. Poland's naval aggression around Lhasa continues - Battleship/Dreadnought fleet threatening allied city-state. Poland has nuclear capability (5 Uranium, 13,891 military) and declared war despite previous alliance. Lhasa under naval bombardment with 5 Polish Battleships threatening our ally. Heightening hostility directs AI to prioritize Poland as secondary conquest target after Shoshone cultural elimination. Not full escalation yet but clear warning that Poland's aggression has consequences.
        - UpdatedTurn: 417
        
        ### England
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: INCREASE ENGLAND HOSTILITY to maximum (-50/-50) during capitulation phase. England at 52% war score with 100% war weariness; they also possess nuclear capability. Accelerating capitulation removes England's 19 UN votes (critical if Shoshone cultural victory fails), eliminates nuclear threat, and allows full focus on Shoshone. Oxford almost razed (1 population), London heavily damaged. No compromise - demand complete surrender. England's warmongering threat against France must end permanently.
        - UpdatedTurn: 419
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: INCREASE HOSTILITY vs Shoshone to -25/-25 from -20/-20 in response to Moson Kahni producing Atomic Bomb this turn. Shoshone now has nuclear weapons capability alongside their cultural victory threat (4/5 civs influenced). France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers this turn, showing significant naval threat. Combined cultural AND nuclear threat requires elevated diplomatic hostility to signal France will respond decisively. Shoshone's 3621 tourism and new nuclear arsenal make them an existential threat requiring maximum military pressure.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: CRITICAL ADJUSTMENT: Shoshone produced Atomic Bomb this turn - Nuke stockpiling priority. Naval increased to 95 (from 90) - France lost 2 Battleships to Shoshone Fleet Destroyers, naval superiority must be reestablished. Antiair 95 (from 90) - Shoshone has Heavy Bombers, air defense critical. UseNuke remains 95 - Shoshone nuclear escalation may require preemptive or retaliatory nuclear strikes. MilitaryTraining 95 (from 90) - continuous unit upgrades essential. Offense 95 / Mobile 100 maintained - aggressive ground campaigns against Shoshone wonder cities needed. Defense reduced to 70 (from 75) - shift resources to offense. Espionage 90 - Poland spy detected in巴黎, Brazil spy in Orleans, Shoshone nuclear production requires intelligence.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - French Sphere of Influence: Venezia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Sanction: The Shoshone:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Melbourne:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Travel Ban:
            - Description: Restricts international travel between Civilizations. Reduces the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 35%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Palatium:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Bornu:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - French Sphere of Influence: Sydney:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 408
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Brandenburg:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: England
          - Enact: French Sphere of Influence: Ecbatana:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: France
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: The Shoshone

  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Free Thought (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -5 Unhappiness from Religious Unrest in all Cities.
        - * +25% Instant Yields from Great Scientists.
        - * Earn Great Scientists +33% faster.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Justice (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - * Cities with a garrison gain +25% Ranged Combat Strength.
        - * Royal Guardhouse built in Capital (+3 Production, +2 Defense, +50 Hit Points, +10% Military Supply from Population in all Cities, 1 Engineer Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):

        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.

However, to avoid catastrophic humanitarian harm, I am drastically lowering first-strike nuclear probability while keeping arsenals for deterrence. Our -57 GPT deficit and rising war weariness demand higher prioritization of Gold and Happiness. A modest Culture increase supports our score lead in the likely event this becomes a Time contest against The Shoshone.

It also locks our vassals against foreign liberation attempts.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation as Napoleon of France in this Civilization V (Vox Populi) game.
            
            **Current State Assessment:**
            
            1. **Victory Progress:**
               - **Domination:** I'm in the lead with 37% of capitals controlled (3/8: Istanbul, Sukhothai, Paris). Poland and Shoshone each have 25%.
               - **Science:** The Shoshone have completed Apollo (1). I have 0 parts. I'm behind here.
               - **Cultural:** I'm influential over 4/5 civs (missing only The Shoshone at Familiar 27%). The Shoshone are influential over 4/5 civs too, but I need to check if they have the ideology and wonder requirements.
               - **Diplomatic:** I have 33 delegates (75% victory progress). Need 44. Voting in 4 turns. I'm the current leader.
            
            2. **Military Situation:**
               - Currently at war with: The Shoshone, England, Brazil, Poland.
               - Military Strength: 62,974 (strong)
               - War weariness: 18% vs Shoshone, 50% vs England, 14% vs Brazil, 0% vs Poland.
               - Tactical zones show I have strong forces near my homeland. England is at -52% war score and 100% war weariness - they're nearly broken.
               - Poland is at only 2% war score vs me with 0% war weariness.
               - The Shoshone war is at -6% score with 18% war weariness.
            
            3. **Economy:**
               - Gold: 88
               - GPT: -57 (concerning, but manageable given total gold reserves)
               - Science: 2303/turn
               - Culture: 2386/turn
               - Tourism: 1478/turn
               - Happiness: 55% (not great but stable)
            
            4. **Diplomacy/UN:**
               - World Ideology: Autocracy (my ideology).
               - Several French Spheres of Influence enacted.
               - England proposing Decolonization; I'm proposing Ecbatana sphere; Shoshone proposing International Worlds.
               - I lead delegates at 33, but need 44.
            
            5. **Strategic Technologies:**
               - Currently researching Stealth (1 turn left).
               - Next tech was set to Electronics.
            
            6. **Policies:**
               - Next policy in 22 turns.
               - Currently have extensive policy trees including Autocracy, Artistry, etc.
               - Next policy was None.
            
            **Strategic Analysis:**
            
            Given that this is turn 421 of 500, we're in the late game. I need to evaluate which victory is most achievable:
            
            1. **Diplomatic Victory:** I'm closest here with 33/44 delegates (75%). Voting in 4 turns. If I can secure 11 more delegates, I win. However, I don't know if I can get there in 4 turns. I have many city-state allies already. The Decolonization proposal from England could hurt me significantly by reducing my city-state influence to 50.
            
            2. **Domination:** I need 5 more capitals. I have the military strength (62k). But The Shoshone also have strong military (61k) and Poland has decent strength too.
            
            3. **Cultural:** I'm influential over 4/5 civs. The Shoshone is the only holdout at Familiar (27%). Since they also have high culture output, this might take a while.
            
            4. **Science:** The Shoshone already have Apollo. I'm way behind.
            
            **Immediate Concerns:**
            - The **Decolonization** proposal is extremely dangerous to my diplomatic victory path. If passed, it would reduce my influence with all non-permanent-ally city-states to 50, likely costing me many allies.
            - England is proposing it. I need to make sure it fails or deal with England.
            - England is basically broken (100% war weariness, -52% war score). I should probably make peace with England to reduce war weariness and focus elsewhere. Actually, looking at the war score, *I'm* winning against England 52% with 50% war weariness. Wait, no: "War (Our Score: 52%; Our War Weariness: 50%)" - this means France has 52% war score and 50% war weariness. England's data says "War (Our Score: -52%; Our War Weariness: 100%)" - so England is at 100% war weariness. This means England would likely accept peace on very favorable terms. But taking English cities might help domination, or I could just sign peace to eliminate their war weariness cost (which might be contributing to my happiness issues? No, war weariness affects happiness in the enemy lands usually, but in VP it affects happiness globally).
            
            Actually, I should seriously consider making peace with England since they're broken, and focus my military on either:
            - Poland (war score is near even, but I'm stronger)
            - The Shoshone (my main rival in multiple victory types)
            
            Wait, I need to look at my war weariness. 50% is very high. That means my citizens are unhappy about the war. But my happiness is 55% which is okay.
            
            **Key Decision Points:**
            
            1. **Technology:** Stealth completes in 1 turn. Electronics is queued next. Electronics unlocks Police Station (security) and leads to Alternative Energy/Telecommunications. Given I'm going for Diplomatic or Domination, is this the right path?
               - If I want to push for Science to catch up, maybe I should research something else. But I'm not going for science victory realistically.
               - Electronics -> Telecommunications could help with Diplomatic victory (additional delegate routes?) Actually Telecommunications unlocks the United Nations if it's not already built... but it says World Ideology: Autocracy is active, so UN exists. Telecommunications might give better espionage or something.
               - Computers (grants trade route, Carrier) could be useful.
            
            Actually, looking at my current research "Stealth" - this unlocks Modern era techs. Let me reconsider. I'm an Information Era civ (Era: Information). Stealth is probably an Information Era tech.
            
            Wait, the options list Technologies as:
            - Electronics
            - Computers
            
            These are the only strategic tech options available to set. Electronics is already set. Computers might be better for trade routes and carriers. But Electronics is fine.
            
            2. **Policy:** I need to choose a next policy. Available policies include:
               - Futurism (Autocracy level 1 tenet) - strengthens tourism, great works
               - New World Order (Autocracy level 1 tenet) - happiness, faster police stations
               - Iron Fist (Autocracy level 1 tenet) - vassal benefits
               - Various continuing branch policies: Imperium, Regimental System, Rights of Man, Honor, Civilizing Mission, Free Thought, Ceremony, Colonialism, Justice, Liberty, Organization, Nobility, Free Trade, Division of Labor, Trade Confederacy, Shadow Networks
            
            I already have many Autocracy policies. Let me check what's missing. I have: Elite Forces, Military-Industrial Complex, United Front, Lebensraum, Air Supremacy, Lightning Warfare, Mare Nostrum, Co-Prosperity Sphere, Martial Spirit. That's 9 policies in Autocracy.
            
            For Ideology tenets, I can take Level 1 tenets if I haven't filled all 3 slots. Wait, Autocracy tenets: Level 1 has 3 slots (Futurism, New World Order, Iron Fist). I have 9 policies, but those might be spread across levels. Actually in VP, ideology trees have tiers. I don't know which tier each belongs to, but the options suggest Futurism, New World Order, and Iron Fist are all "Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy". 
            
            Given my diplomatic situation, **New World Order** would be helpful: -2 Unhappiness from Distress, faster Police Stations. But I already have a lot of Autocracy.
            For domination, **Iron Fist** helps with vassal yields (I have Siam as vassal).
            
            Actually, **Shadow Networks** (Statecraft) gives +25% science from allied city-states, +3% culture in capital per spy points. Given I have many city-state allies, this is huge for science and culture.
            
            **Trade Confederacy** (Statecraft) gives +25% yields from International Trade Routes and influence from city-state trade routes. Also strong.
            
            **Free Thought** (Rationalism) gives +25% instant yields from Great Scientists and +33% Great Scientist generation. Good for catching up in science.
            
            But looking at my current path... I need the NEXT policy. Since I have 22 turns until next policy, this isn't urgent right now. But I should set it now if possible.
            
            3. **Grand Strategy/Flavors:** Currently set to Conquest with extremely high military flavors (Offense 95, Mobilization 100, etc.) and Nuke/UseNuke at 95/100.
            
            Given that I'm at war with multiple opponents and leading in delegates, should I continue full conquest or pivot slightly?
            
            **War Situation with Poland:**
            - Poland military: 36,788
            - My military: 62,974
            - War score: 2% for Poland, 0% weariness
            - They have many cities (19). Taking their capital (Warsaw) would give me another capital for domination.
            - But they're far away based on the tactical zone reports (enemy zones 4468, 4552 are distant).
            
            **War Situation with The Shoshone:**
            - Military: 61,029 (very close to mine)
            - War score: -6%, weariness 18%
            - They have Apollo program! I need to stop them if going for science denial, but I'm not well-positioned.
            - They have 16 cities.
            - Distance: Far.
            
            **War Situation with England:**
            - Military: 2,344 vs my 62,974. They're trivial.
            - War score: 52%, weariness 50%.
            - They have 8 cities.
            - But they proposed Decolonization which threatens my diplomatic victory.
            
            Given England's proposal of Decolonization, I have two options:
            1. Kill England entirely to stop their proposals (but that takes time, and warmonger penalties)
            2. Ignore it and try to get enough delegates anyway
            3. Make peace with England and maybe they'll stop? No, they already proposed it.
            
            Wait, the proposal is already made. It'll be voted on in 4 turns. Decolonization "lowers the Influence of all Opponents with all City-States to 50, unless they are permanent allies." I have many permanent allies via Sphere of Influence! Let me check:
            - French Sphere of Influence: Venezia, Melbourne, Bornu, Hanoi, Ecbatana, Palatium, Sydney. These are PERMANENT allies.
            - So Decolonization won't affect those! It would only affect non-permanent allies like maybe Lhasa? Let me check Lhasa: "Ally (Influence: 749, Distance: Far, Protected)" - not a sphere, so influence would drop to 50.
            
            If Decolonization passes, I would lose most of my non-permanent city-state allies. But I keep the spheres. How many delegates do I get from spheres vs regular allies?
            
            Actually, looking at my delegates: 33. The Shoshone have 24, England 19. If decolonization passes, many civs lose city-state allies. But I keep my permanent ones. This might actually *help* me relative to others, since I have so many spheres of influence! Others would lose their regular allies.
            
            However, the Shoshone have a Sphere of Influence on Brandenburg too. And England has one on Utica. Poland doesn't seem to have spheres.
            
            Wait, if Decolonization passes, my influence with allied city-states drops to 50 (Friend level, not Ally). But my Sphere of Influence city-states stay as permanent allies. Let me count my current allies:
            - Permanent (Spheres): Venezia, Melbourne, Bornu, Hanoi, Ecbatana, Palatium, Sydney (7) - these survive.
            - Regular allies: Lhasa - would drop to 50.
            Wait, is that all? Let me check the list again.
            - Bornu: Ally (sphere)
            - Hanoi: Ally (sphere)
            - Ecbatana: Ally (sphere)
            - Venezia: Ally (sphere)
            - Lhasa: Ally (protected, no sphere)
            - Palatium: Ally (sphere)
            - Sydney: Ally (sphere)
            - Melbourne: Ally (sphere)
            
            So I might only lose Lhasa if Decolonization passes. That wouldn't be terrible! Actually it could be good if it hurts The Shoshone and Poland more.
            
            But wait, the proposal is from England. If I vote against it, it might fail? Or I could let it pass since it doesn't hurt me much.
            
            Actually, I need to think about what happens in 4 turns at the World Congress vote.
            
            **Grand Strategy:**
            I should probably maintain Conquest but maybe slightly reduce some military flavors to improve economy/happiness, or keep them high since I'm actively at war.
            
            But wait - I have a path to Diplomatic Victory. I need 11 more delegates. Where can I get them?
